+   844	// valueSchema retains literal object keys without guessing computed values.
+   845	func (s *scanner) valueSchema(e ast.Expr, vars map[string]ast.Expr, src *source) Object {
+   846		if lit, ok := e.(*ast.CompositeLit); ok {
+   847			if _, ok := lit.Type.(*ast.MapType); ok {
+   848				props := Object{}
+   849				for _, el := range lit.Elts {
+   850					kv, ok := el.(*ast.KeyValueExpr)
+   851					if !ok {
+   852						return s.schema(lit.Type, src, 0)
+   853					}
+   854					key, ok := kv.Key.(*ast.BasicLit)
+   855					if !ok || key.Kind != token.STRING {
+   856						return s.schema(lit.Type, src, 0)
+   857					}
+   858					name, err := strconv.Unquote(key.Value)
+   859					if err != nil {
+   860						return Object{"x-body": "unresolved"}
+   861					}
+   862					props[name] = s.valueSchema(kv.Value, vars, src)
+   863				}
+   864				return Object{"type": "object", "properties": props}
+   865			}
+   866			return s.schema(lit.Type, src, 0)
+   867		}
+   868		if id, ok := e.(*ast.Ident); ok {
+   869			if id.Name == "true" || id.Name == "false" {
+   870				return Object{"type": "boolean"}
+   871			}
+   872			if id.Name == "nil" {
+   873				return Object{"type": "null"}
+   874			}
+   875			return s.schema(vars[id.Name], src, 0)
+   876		}
+   877		if lit, ok := e.(*ast.BasicLit); ok {
+   878			switch lit.Kind {
+   879			case token.STRING:
+   880				return Object{"type": "string"}
+   881			case token.INT:
+   882				return Object{"type": "integer"}
+   883			case token.FLOAT:
+   884				return Object{"type": "number"}
+   885			}
+   886		}
+   887		return Object{"x-body": "unresolved"}

+CLAUDE.md
+# CLAUDE.md
+
+Guidance for engineering agents working in `onesyue/yueboard`, an independent
+repository in the Yue.to workspace. Read `../CLAUDE.md` for workspace rules.
+Commit and push this repository separately.
+
+## Purpose and compatibility
+
+**Native Go control plane for Yue.to.** The strangler rewrite originated from
+Xboard, but production Xboard was retired on 2026-07-23. There is no PHP process,
+container or fallback. PostgreSQL uses native names such as `users`, `orders`,
+`plugins` and `settings`. Released clients retain the compatible HTTP wire
+contract in `docs/api-contract.md`.
+
+1. **Preserve client contracts.** The v1 envelope remains
+   `{status,message,data,error}` through `internal/platform/apiresp` where that
+   contract applies. Authentication rejection must be HTTP 403 with non-HTML
+   JSON: clients use non-HTML 401/403 responses to detect logout. Check the
+   contract's endpoint-specific exceptions before changing responses.
+2. **Never restore the retired panel fallback.** `internal/platform/fallback`
+   was deleted and legacy fallback environment variables are unsupported.
+   Unmatched routes use the anti-GFW decoy/404 behavior. Implement missing
+   business endpoints natively with contract coverage.
+
+## Commands
+
+```sh
+go run ./cmd/yueboard          # :8080; health-only mode without DATABASE_URL
+go build ./... && go vet ./...
+go test ./...
+go test ./internal/modules/emby/...
+go test ./internal/modules/emby/ -run TestToUsername -v
+gofmt -w <file>
+```
+
+## PostgreSQL integration tests
+
+Integration tests use in-process `httptest` servers and a real PostgreSQL schema
+fixture derived from production `pg_dump`. They catch execution-only errors such
+as ambiguous columns, unused INSERT parameters and pgx fatal-close on mismatched
+types. Fixtures include PG17/18 `transaction_timeout`, requiring PostgreSQL 17+.
+The actual CI environment uses PostgreSQL 18.
+
+```sh
+YUEBOARD_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://REDACTED \
+  go test -tags integration -v ./test/integration/...
+```
+
+**That command does not cover the module integration suites.** `nodesync`,
+`emby`, `handoff`, `deviceidentity`, `user`, `referencelock`, `order`, `db` and
+`nativeproto` require their own coverage. Missing DSNs can call `t.Skip` while
+`go test` still prints `ok`; inspect actual verbose run/pass/skip events.
+`TestClonedReportOutboxCannotDoubleBill` and the billing-report cases are among
+the critical tests outside `./test/integration/`.
+
+The public central runner owns the current sequencing. Run domain fixtures on
+a fresh PostgreSQL service, the clean-PG gate in an independent fresh database
+of the same cluster, module suites serially, then full db/nativeproto migrations
+and production-scale cases. Earlier migrations can create roles that hide a
+fixture-initialization defect. `deviceidentity` and `nodesync` rebuild `public`
+and must follow consumers of the full fixture.
+
+For a targeted module run against an explicitly disposable database:
+
+```sh
+YUEBOARD_TRAFFIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://REDACTED \
+YUEBOARD_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://REDACTED \
+YUEBOARD_EMAILVERIFY_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://REDACTED \
+YUEBOARD_TEST_FIXTURES=$PWD/test/fixtures \
+YUEBOARD_MIGRATION_TEST_ALLOW_RESET=YES_I_UNDERSTAND_THIS_DATABASE_IS_DISPOSABLE \
+  go test -count=1 -p 1 -tags integration -v \
+    ./internal/modules/user ./internal/platform/referencelock \
+    ./internal/modules/order ./internal/modules/emby ./internal/modules/handoff \
+    ./internal/platform/deviceidentity ./internal/modules/nodesync
+```
+
+`user` and `referencelock` were missing from all commands before 2026-09-21.
+`nativeproto` uses the migration DSN. `internal/platform/toolchain`'s
+`TestEveryIntegrationPackageRunsInCI` detects integration packages omitted from
+CI orchestration. A successful package line alone is insufficient.
+
+Migration tests need separate explicit inputs:
+
+```sh
+YUEBOARD_MIGRATION_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgres://REDACTED \
+YUEBOARD_MIGRATION_TEST_ALLOW_RESET=YES_I_UNDERSTAND_THIS_DATABASE_IS_DISPOSABLE \
+YUEBOARD_MIGRATION_TEST_PRODUCTION_SCALE=1 \
+  go test -count=1 -tags integration -v ./internal/platform/db ./internal/platform/nativeproto
+```
+
+These tests are destructive (`DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE`). Use the native
+runner's ordering or a separate disposable database, never production.
+`TestNativeMigrationsProductionScaleOnPostgres` executes the complete goose
+chain from zero, including the approximately 1.1-million-row scale fixture.
+`TestNativeMigrationsOnPostgres` checks a selected chain's ACL, trigger and
+catalog semantics. An ACL migration requires updated semantic assertions, not
+just adding its filename. The 2026-08-12 `emby_launch_audit` permission failure
+exposed the cost of skipped real PostgreSQL migration tests.
+
+The shared integration account/server also shares the checkout rate limiter.
+Production defaults to ten checkouts per user per minute. `TestMain` defaults
+`YUEBOARD_CHECKOUT_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` to `10000` before `bootHandler`, unless
+the caller sets it, to prevent unrelated later cases receiving 429. Keep the
+production default protected by `checkout_rate_limit_default_test.go`.
+`t.Setenv` inside a test cannot reconfigure a limiter already read by `order.New`.
+
+Use workspace `../scripts/run-yueboard-pg18-gauntlet.sh` when no suitable local
+PostgreSQL is running. It uses disposable `postgres:18.6-alpine`, a pool size of
+four and automatic container cleanup. Do not run heavy tests on the bastion:
+it has been CPU-steal constrained since the dated 2026-10-11 observation.
+Clean up disposable containers, binaries and accounts. Production database
+inspection remains read-only; never use it for destructive tests.
+
+## Architecture
+
+The backend is a modular monolith using chi and pgx/pgxpool, with no ORM.
+
+- `internal/modules/<mod>/`: business domains with `New(pool, st, ...)` and
+  `Routes(chi.Router)`. Do not import another business module.
+- `internal/platform/<pkg>/`: shared `db`, `auth`, `settings`, `apiresp`, `mail`,
+  `verify`, `metrics`, `antigfw`, `resource` and `apptime` infrastructure.
+- `internal/server/server.go`: composition only. Middleware order matters:
+  `antigfw.Harden` first (`Server: nginx`), then `RealIP`,
+  `antigfw.TarpitMiddleware`, RequestID, Recoverer and metrics.
+- `cmd/yueboard/main.go`: constructs `server.Deps`. Wire new modules here and
+  mirror the wiring in `test/integration/harness_test.go::bootHandler`.
+  `deps.Extra` is for `/api`; `deps.Root` is for root endpoints.
+
+User modules mount only under `/api/v1/*`, using relative paths such as
+`/user/emby` in `Routes`. `/api/v2/*` contains the admin group and subscription
+aliases, not user APIs. `TestAdminRoutesGuarded` protects that boundary after
+stale dual-prefix guidance was corrected on 2026-09-05.
+
+Admin traffic uses `/api/v2/{secure_path}/*` inside the `/api` group, never the
+root group. The production secure path exists only in settings; do not record
+it in source. Subscription delivery and node synchronization use `deps.Root`.
+For explicitly parallel development, each worker owns its package; the main
+integrator alone wires `main.go` and `harness_test.go`.
+
+## Schema and generated data access
+
+- Goose migrations live in `internal/platform/db/migrations/*.sql` and are
+  embedded. Run `yueboard migrate up|down|status`; service startup does not
+  auto-migrate. `adoptBaseline` recognizes existing `users` and stamps version
+  1 without rerunning 00001. Actual column conversion begins at 00002.
+- Named SQL in `internal/modules/<mod>/queries/` generates that module's `store/`
+  via sqlc. Never hand-edit generated code or import another module's store.
+  Shared `plugins`, `settings` and `personal_access_tokens` stores belong in
+  the corresponding platform package.
+- Unmarked migrations must expand: add tables, nullable/defaulted columns,
+  indexes, functions or grants. `DROP TABLE|COLUMN|FUNCTION`, `RENAME`,
+  `ALTER … TYPE` and `SET NOT NULL` without a default require
+  `-- +yue contract-of: NNNNN` at the Up header, with NNNNN no greater than the
+  deployed `schema-compat.txt` floor. Advance that floor only after actual
+  successful deployment. `make check-migrations`, PG18 integration and runtime
+  `migrate up` enforce the same rule. The exemption set is capped at 83.
+- `test/fixtures/schema.sql` is the shared real-DDL source for sqlc and
+  integration. Update it with column migrations. `make generate` pins sqlc;
+  `make check-generated` rejects regeneration drift. Query native nullable
+  timestamps, preserve NULL through pgtype and convert epochs at response time.
+- Keep `resource.PlanResource`, `apptime` (Asia/Shanghai), the v1 envelope and
+  403-JSON logout semantics, and anti-GFW nginx-404 behavior. These are client
+  and domain contracts, not disposable PHP migration leftovers. A future owned
+  `/api/v3` requires coordinated client/token migration, separate from schema work.
+
+## Required workspace skills
+
+Do not split this file merely to reduce its apparent size; shared procedures
+already live in the workspace `.claude/skills/` and are linked for both agents.
+
+- `pg-safety`: migrations, grants, role manifests, cross-owner reads and monetary
+  eligibility. Required privileges exceed the statement verb: DELETE predicates
+  need SELECT, and `ON CONFLICT (a,b) DO UPDATE` needs table-level SELECT.
+- `release-chain`: default-branch pushes participate in the release process.
+- `guard-authoring`: guards, assertions and contract checks must detect faults.
+- Also use `node-lifecycle`, `fleet-diagnose`, `generated-blocks` and
+  `round-closeout` when their scope applies.
+
+## Incident lessons
+
+- pgx v5 `rows.Scan` type mismatches call `rows.fatal()` and silently truncate
+  the rowset. Match real column types, including nullable values and floating
+  monetary columns. Real-schema integration is essential.
+- Plugin configuration is JSON text in `plugins.config`, not `settings`.
+  `invitealias`/`emby` use `SELECT is_enabled, config FROM plugins WHERE code=$1`
+  with a 60-second process cache. Preserve permissive legacy bool/integer forms
+  such as `true`, `1`, `"1"` and `"3"` where the existing contract requires them.
+- PHP `preg_replace` without `/u` operates on bytes; Go regexp operates on runes.
+  `emby.toUsername` uses a byte loop for compatibility. Derived keys/hashes must
+  preserve the original `config('app.key')` string, including `base64:`. Compare
+  PHP `hash_hmac` output and verify the real external-service consumer.
+- Prefix package-level identifiers within modules (`ce`, `ia`, `emby`, etc.) to
+  avoid cross-file and future package-merging collisions.
+
+## Anti-GFW behavior
+
+Read `docs/anti-gfw.md` before changing `internal/platform/antigfw`. Active
+probing motivates `Server: nginx`, nginx-404 responses for invalid unauthenticated
+subscription/node tokens, probe-IP tarpits and optional `YUEBOARD_DECOY_HTML`.
+Do not expose endpoint fingerprints such as `token is error`.
+**API requests carrying Authorization still require JSON 403 for client logout.**
+Decoy 404 applies to unauthenticated subscription/node/unmatched routes.
+
+## Canonical node contract
+
+`proto/yuenode/v1/yuenode.proto` is canonical. The Node copy is an exact mirror,
+protected by `proto/CANONICAL.sha256`, `scripts/sync-proto.sh` and Go tests.
+Change Board first, synchronize Node, run `buf generate` on both sides and
+commit proto plus generated code in each repository. Ordinary builds consume
+tracked generated code and do not require buf.
+
+`scripts/ci/proto-gate.sh` runs lint and breaking checks against
+`proto/breaking-baseline.binpb`. Additive fields/RPCs are permitted. A wire break
+requires upgrading the complete affected fleet before accepting a new baseline
+with `--accept-baseline` and recording the rationale. Do not copy an old fleet
+count from this document. Divergent `machine_id` comments once gave the two
+proto files and generated code three meanings despite identical field numbers;
+green compilation does not prove semantic agreement.
+

+
+# Guard authoring (Yue.to)
+
+The expensive failure is a green guard protecting nothing. The linked incident examples
+occurred in actual production work.
+
+Apply the checklist to the guard's actual inputs, dependencies and claimed
+coverage. Running an existing check does not require redesigning it. A changed
+assertion, scan boundary or success/failure classification does require this skill.
+For import/fixture leakage, load [test isolation](references/test-isolation.md)
+first; use the guard checklist only if guard coverage changes.
+
+Before adding an invariant, establish which consumer needs it and check its
+actual semantics. Challenge it with one realistic counterexample at that boundary:
+subprocess execution for locks, a write failure for persistence, or raw single-target
+output for collectors. Matching strings or unrelated runner-image labels cannot
+establish those properties. Reuse the recorded interpreter/image, user and mounts;
+classify failures as startup, collection or assertion before editing product code.
+
+## Establish three facts before accepting green
+
+1. Did this run reach the target? Zero executions is not a pass.
+2. Did the scan cover the intended objects? Zero matches is not universal success.
+3. Is the guarded layer the layer users and production actually encounter?
+
+A negative answer makes the green result meaningless.
+
+## Load only the relevant incident reference
+
+Keep the common criteria and checklist below for guard changes. Read a reference
+only when its failure class applies; the catalog is retained evidence, not an
+extra audit assignment.
+
+| Observed problem | Reference |
+|---|---|
+| A check never executes, scans nothing, targets the wrong layer, hides failure, or has no legitimate success path | [Coverage failures](references/coverage-failures.md), numbered incidents 1–9 |
+| Wrong interpreter or checkout, SSH stdin, toolchain drift, signed installation closure, systemd results, incomplete pagination or runtime budgets | [Execution boundaries](references/execution-boundaries.md) |
+| Import/fixture pollution, swallowed real I/O timeouts, fork deadlocks or teardown order | [Test isolation](references/test-isolation.md) |
+
+## Required guard-delivery checklist
+
+- [ ] Remove/rename/delete the protected object in an isolated mutation: the
+  guard must fail, not mistake missing input for correctness.
+- [ ] Exercise dependency failures the guard can encounter: wrong credentials
+  for authenticated probes, network failure for remote probes, and permission
+  failure for protected inputs. Verify their visible classifications.
+- [ ] For scans, require a minimum target count; for a single target, prove arrival.
+- [ ] Before counting negative observations, prove target-handler arrival and
+  run controls protecting legitimate behavior.
+- [ ] Register cross-repository guards in `scripts/check-all-contracts.sh`.
+  `<path>` must name the sibling file actually read, not the guard script itself;
+  otherwise missing-repository SKIP is unreachable. `guard-ci-claims` enforces it.
+- [ ] Enumerate every criterion dimension, such as status × amount × ownership.
+- [ ] Never duplicate live fleet counts. Since 2026-09-14, derive them from
+  `servers.deploy_mode='direct'`; node-control-auth-migrate.py EXPECTED baselines
+  no longer exist. Documentation may retain dated snapshots. Only mechanically
+  enforced authoritative projections and date-anchored observations are exceptions.
+
+## Workspace-only guards
+
+`scripts/check-all-contracts.sh` alone knows the complete cross-repository guard
+set. Single-repo CI cannot see all private siblings; a green component does not
+establish a shared contract. The registry explicitly states whether a component
+CI consumer exists.
+
+A 2026-08-02 isolated Ops checkout showed whole tests skipped in ci-backend.yml:
+`panel-deep-links` (2), `idempotency-catalog` (5, module-level pytestmark),
+`registry-links` (2), and cross-surface design-token-gate cases. These and the
+root scripts-level comparators run only in the workspace. Run the contract script
+before pushing cross-repository changes.
+
+`guard-ci-claims` (`scripts/check-guard-ci-claims.py`) fails when sibling-dependent
+entries lack workspace-only marking or claimed component CI lacks a workflow
+execution path. Missing repositories must report SKIP; `<path>` identifies the
+actual sibling input, never the guard itself, so missing input cannot appear PASS.
+
+`scripts/gauntlet.sh` similarly turns a former verbal pre-release promise into
+execution: missing tools/repos are SKIP, never PASS, and too few actually executed
+stages fail the gate.
+

+
+**Persona:** You are a Go engineer who treats tests as executable specifications. You write tests to constrain behavior, not to hit coverage targets.
+
+**Thinking mode:** Reason as thoroughly as possible for test strategy design and failure analysis — shallow reasoning misses edge cases and produces brittle tests that pass today but break tomorrow. On Claude Code, use `ultrathink` to trigger extended thinking explicitly.
+
+**Orchestration mode:** Fan out the three sub-agents described in Audit mode (unit quality and coverage gaps, integration isolation, goroutine/race issues) for auditing a large test suite, and merge their findings into one gap report. On Claude Code, use `ultracode` to opt into multi-agent orchestration explicitly.
+
+**Modes:**
+
+- **Write mode** — generating new tests for existing or new code. Work sequentially through the code under test; use `gotests` to scaffold table-driven tests, then enrich with edge cases and error paths.
+- **Review mode** — reviewing a PR's test changes. Focus on the diff: check coverage of new behaviour, assertion quality, table-driven structure, and absence of flakiness patterns. Sequential.
+- **Audit mode** — auditing an existing test suite for gaps, flakiness, or bad patterns (order-dependent tests, missing `t.Parallel()`, implementation-detail coupling). Launch up to 3 parallel sub-agents split by concern: (1) unit test quality and coverage gaps, (2) integration test isolation and build tags, (3) goroutine leaks and race conditions.
+- **Debug mode** — a test is failing or flaky. Work sequentially: reproduce reliably, isolate the failing assertion, trace the root cause in production code or test setup.
+
+> **Community default.** A company skill that explicitly supersedes `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-testing` skill takes precedence.
+
+**Dependencies:**
+
+- gotests: `go install github.com/cweill/gotests/gotests@latest`
+
+# Go Testing Best Practices
+
+This skill guides the creation of production-ready tests for Go applications. Follow these principles to write maintainable, fast, and reliable tests.
+
+## Best Practices Summary
+
+1. Table-driven tests MUST use named subtests -- every test case needs a `name` field passed to `t.Run`
+2. Integration tests MUST use build tags (`//go:build integration`) to separate from unit tests
+3. Tests MUST NOT depend on execution order -- each test MUST be independently runnable
+4. Independent tests SHOULD use `t.Parallel()` when possible
+5. Tests MUST assert observable behavior and public API contracts, not implementation details -- a test coupled to internals turns every refactor into a test rewrite while proving nothing about the contract
+6. Packages with goroutines SHOULD use `goleak.VerifyTestMain` in `TestMain` to detect goroutine leaks
+7. Use testify as helpers, not a replacement for standard library
+8. Mock interfaces, not concrete types
+9. Keep unit tests fast (< 1ms), use build tags for integration tests
+10. Run tests with race detection in CI
+11. Include examples as executable documentation
+12. Test files MUST be named after the source file under test, not after the function or method being tested
+13. Test functions SHOULD appear in the same order as the functions/methods they test in the source file
+
+## Test Structure and Organization
+
+### File Conventions
+
+```go
+// package_test.go - tests in same package (white-box, access unexported)
+package mypackage
+
+// mypackage_test.go - tests in test package (black-box, public API only)
+package mypackage_test
+```
+
+Name the test file after the source file it tests, not after the function or method under test. Go's convention is one test file per source file (`foo.go` -> `foo_test.go`), because tools (`go test`, coverage reports, IDE "jump to test" navigation, `gotests`) and reviewers all resolve tests by source file, not by symbol. A source file usually declares several functions/methods; splitting its tests by symbol name scatters them across many files and breaks that file-to-file mapping.
+
+```
+// ✓ Good — one test file per source file
+helloworld.go       -> helloworld_test.go   // contains TestHelloWorld, TestAbcd, TestXyz, ...
+
+// ✗ Bad — test file named after the function/method instead of the source file
+helloworld.go       -> abcd_test.go         // wrong: should be helloworld_test.go
+```
+
+Exception: very large source files MAY be split into multiple `_test.go` files by concern (e.g. `foo_test.go` + `foo_edgecases_test.go`), but each split file's name MUST still be derived from the source file name, never from an individual function name. Prefer keeping a single `_test.go` file per source file even when it grows large — splitting adds navigation overhead and is rarely worth it; reach for the exception only when a single file becomes genuinely unwieldy to browse or review.
+
+Within a test file, order test functions to match the order their tested functions/methods appear in the source file. A reader (human or agent) scrolling `foo.go` alongside `foo_test.go` can then find the matching test by position instead of searching; drift between the two orderings compounds every time either file grows.
+
+### Naming Conventions
+
+```go
+func TestAdd(t *testing.T) { ... }               // function test
+func TestMyStruct_MyMethod(t *testing.T) { ... } // method test
+func BenchmarkAdd(b *testing.B) { ... }          // benchmark
+func ExampleAdd() { ... }                        // example
+func FuzzAdd(f *testing.F) { ... }               // fuzz test
+```
+
+## Table-Driven Tests
+
+Table-driven tests are the idiomatic Go way to test multiple scenarios. Always name each test case.
+
+```go
+func TestCalculatePrice(t *testing.T) {
+    tests := []struct {
+        name     string
+        quantity int
+        unitPrice float64
+        expected  float64
+    }{
+        {
+            name:      "single item",
+            quantity:  1,
+            unitPrice: 10.0,
+            expected:  10.0,
+        },
+        {
+            name:      "bulk discount - 100 items",
+            quantity:  100,
+            unitPrice: 10.0,
+            expected:  900.0, // 10% discount
+        },
+        {
+            name:      "zero quantity",
+            quantity:  0,
+            unitPrice: 10.0,
+            expected:  0.0,
+        },
+    }
+
+    for _, tt := range tests {
+        t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
+            got := CalculatePrice(tt.quantity, tt.unitPrice)
+            if got != tt.expected {
+                t.Errorf("CalculatePrice(%d, %.2f) = %.2f, want %.2f",
+                    tt.quantity, tt.unitPrice, got, tt.expected)
+            }
+        })
+    }
+}
+```
+
+## Common Pitfall: Assert Scope Leaking into Subtests
+
+Never create a testify `assert`/`require` instance in the parent test function and reuse it inside `t.Run` closures. `assert.New(t)` captures the exact `*testing.T` it was built with, so if that `t` belongs to the parent, every failure raised inside the subtest gets attributed to the _parent_ test in `go test` output — the failing subtest itself still reports `--- PASS`, silently hiding which case broke. This happens whether or not the subtest calls `t.Parallel()`.
+
+```go
+// WRONG -- `is` is bound to the parent's t
+func TestCalculatePrice(t *testing.T) {
+    is := assert.New(t)
+    for _, tt := range tests {
+        t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
+            is.Equal(tt.expected, CalculatePrice(tt.quantity, tt.unitPrice)) // misattributed on failure
+        })
+    }
+}
+
+// RIGHT -- each subtest builds its own instance from its own t
+func TestCalculatePrice(t *testing.T) {
+    for _, tt := range tests {
+        t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
+            is := assert.New(t)
+            is.Equal(tt.expected, CalculatePrice(tt.quantity, tt.unitPrice))
+        })
+    }
+}
+```
+
+Verify with a deliberately-broken case: if `go test -v -run TestName` shows `--- FAIL: TestName` but every `--- PASS: TestName/subtest_name` line still says PASS, the assert scope is leaking.
+
+## Unit Tests
+
+Unit tests should be fast (< 1ms), isolated (no external dependencies), and deterministic.
+
+## Testing HTTP Handlers
+
+Use `httptest` for handler tests with table-driven patterns. See [HTTP Testing](./references/http-testing.md) for examples with request/response bodies, query parameters, headers, and status code assertions.
+
+## Goroutine Leak Detection with goleak
+
+Use `go.uber.org/goleak` to detect leaking goroutines, especially for concurrent code:
+
+```go
+import (
+    "testing"
+    "go.uber.org/goleak"
+)
+
+func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
+    goleak.VerifyTestMain(m)
+}
+```
+
+To exclude specific goroutine stacks (for known leaks or library goroutines):
+
+```go
+func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
+    goleak.VerifyTestMain(m,
+        goleak.IgnoreCurrent(),
+    )
+}
+```
+
+Or per-test:
+
+```go
+func TestWorkerPool(t *testing.T) {
+    defer goleak.VerifyNone(t)
+    // ... test code ...
+}
+```
+
+## testing/synctest for Deterministic Goroutine Testing
+
+`testing/synctest` (Go 1.25+) provides deterministic tests for goroutines, timers, deadlines, and context cancellation. Time advances only when all goroutines are blocked, making ordering predictable.
+
+When to use `synctest` instead of real time:
+
+- Testing concurrent code with time-based operations (time.Sleep, time.After, time.Ticker)
+- When race conditions need to be reproducible
+- When tests are flaky due to timing issues
+
+```go
+import (
+    "context"
+    "testing"
+    "testing/synctest"
+    "time"
+)
+
+func TestContextTimeout(t *testing.T) {
+    synctest.Test(t, func(t *testing.T) {
+        const timeout = 5 * time.Second
+
+        ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(t.Context(), timeout)
+        defer cancel()
+
+        time.Sleep(timeout - time.Nanosecond)
+        synctest.Wait()
+        if err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {
+            t.Fatalf("before timeout: %v", err)
+        }
+
+        time.Sleep(time.Nanosecond)
+        synctest.Wait()
+        if err := ctx.Err(); err != context.DeadlineExceeded {
+            t.Fatalf("after timeout: got %v, want DeadlineExceeded", err)
+        }
+    })
+}
+```
+
+Use `synctest.Test` in Go 1.25+ and later. Do not use the old Go 1.24 experimental `synctest.Run` API in Go 1.25+ code. If a module explicitly targets Go 1.24 and opts into `GOEXPERIMENT=synctest`, use the old API only as a compatibility fallback.
+
+Key differences in `synctest`:
+
+- `time.Sleep` advances synthetic time instantly when the goroutine blocks
+- `time.After` fires when synthetic time reaches the duration
+- All goroutines run to blocking points before time advances
+- Test execution is deterministic and repeatable
+- Go 1.27+ adds `synctest.Sleep(d)` as a direct helper to advance the bubble's fake clock, equivalent to `time.Sleep(d)` followed by `synctest.Wait()` but without needing a real goroutine to block on
+
+Go 1.27+ also adds `httptest.NewTestServer()`, an in-memory fake-network variant of `httptest.NewServer` that composes with `synctest` — no real socket, so server tests can run inside a `synctest.Test` bubble instead of needing `httptest.NewServer` plus real timers.
+
+## Test Timeouts
+
+For tests that may hang, use a timeout helper that panics with caller location. See [Helpers](./references/helpers.md).
+
+## Benchmarks
+
+Write benchmarks as sub-benchmarks (`b.Run` per variant) so each variant gets its own name in the output — that name is what comparison tooling diffs. For Go 1.24+, use `b.Loop()` rather than a `b.N` loop.
+
+→ See [Benchmarks in a Test Suite](./references/benchmarks.md) for the code shape and size-parameterized examples.
+
+→ See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-benchmark` skill for measurement methodology: `benchstat`, profiling from benchmarks, and CI regression detection.
+
+## Go 1.26+: test artifacts
+
+When a test, benchmark, or fuzz target needs to persist files for inspection, use `ArtifactDir()` instead of ad-hoc paths or repo-local output.
+
+```go
+func TestRenderGoldenArtifact(t *testing.T) {
+    dir := t.ArtifactDir()
+
+    out := filepath.Join(dir, "rendered.json")
+    if err := os.WriteFile(out, renderedBytes, 0o644); err != nil {
+        t.Fatal(err)
+    }
+
+    t.Logf("artifact written: %s", out)
+}
+```
+
+Available on `*testing.T`, `*testing.B`, and `*testing.F` in Go 1.26+.
+
+### Go 1.27+: `stdversion` runs automatically
+
+`go test` now invokes the `stdversion` vet check by default, flagging any use of an API newer than the module's `go` directive. A CI failure from this check means either the `go` directive needs bumping or the code needs to stop using the newer API — it is not a check to silence.
+
+## Parallel Tests
+
+Use `t.Parallel()` to run tests concurrently:
+
+```go
+func TestParallelOperations(t *testing.T) {
+    tests := []struct {
+        name string
+        data []byte
+    }{
+        {"small data", make([]byte, 1024)},
+        {"medium data", make([]byte, 1024*1024)},
+    }
+
+    for _, tt := range tests {
+        t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
+            t.Parallel()
+            is := assert.New(t)
+
+            result := Process(tt.data)
+            is.NotNil(result)
+        })
+    }
+}
+```
+
+## Fuzzing
+
+Use fuzzing to find edge cases and bugs:
+
+```go
+func FuzzReverse(f *testing.F) {
+    f.Add("hello")
+    f.Add("")
+    f.Add("a")
+
+    f.Fuzz(func(t *testing.T, input string) {
+        reversed := Reverse(input)
+        doubleReversed := Reverse(reversed)
+        if input != doubleReversed {
+            t.Errorf("Reverse(Reverse(%q)) = %q, want %q", input, doubleReversed, input)
+        }
+    })
+}
+```
+
+## Examples as Documentation
+
+`ExampleXxx` functions are executable documentation: `go test` compares their stdout to the `// Output:` comment, so a drifting example fails the build instead of misleading readers.
+
+→ See [Examples as Documentation](./references/examples.md) for naming rules, `Unordered output`, and placement.
+
+## Code Coverage
+
+Generate a profile with `go test -coverprofile=coverage.out ./...`, then read the uncovered lines with `go tool cover -html=coverage.out`. Coverage locates untested paths; it does not measure assertion quality, so treat a percentage as a gap finder rather than a target.
+
+→ See [Code Coverage](./references/coverage.md) for coverage modes, `-coverpkg`, and reporting pitfalls.
+
+## Integration Tests
+
+Use build tags to separate integration tests from unit tests:
+
+```go
+//go:build integration
+
+package mypackage
+
+func TestDatabaseIntegration(t *testing.T) {
+    db, err := sql.Open("postgres", os.Getenv("DATABASE_URL"))
+    if err != nil {
+        t.Fatal(err)
+    }
+    defer db.Close()
+
+    // Test real database operations
+}
+```
+
+Run integration tests separately:
+
+```bash
+go test -tags=integration ./...
+```
+
+For Docker Compose fixtures, SQL schemas, and integration test suites, see [Integration Testing](./references/integration-testing.md).
+
+## Mocking
+
+Mock interfaces, not concrete types. Define interfaces where consumed, then create mock implementations.
+
+For mock patterns, test fixtures, and time mocking, see [Mocking](./references/mocking.md).
+
+## Enforce with Linters
+
+Many test best practices are enforced automatically by linters: `thelper`, `paralleltest`, `testifylint`. See the `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-lint` skill for configuration and usage.
+

+## Quick Reference
+
+```bash
+go test ./...                          # all tests
+go test -run TestName ./...            # specific test by exact name
+go test -run TestName/subtest ./...    # subtests within a test
+go test -run 'Test(Add|Sub)' ./...     # multiple tests (regexp OR)
+go test -run 'Test[A-Z]' ./...         # tests starting with capital letter
+go test -run 'TestUser.*' ./...        # tests matching prefix
+go test -run '.*Validation.*' ./...    # tests containing substring
+go test -run TestName/. ./...          # all subtests of TestName
+go test -run '/(unit|integration)' ./... # filter by subtest name
+go test -race ./...                    # race detection
+go test -cover ./...                   # coverage summary
+go test -bench=. -benchmem ./...       # benchmarks
+go test -fuzz=FuzzName ./...           # fuzzing
+go test -tags=integration ./...        # integration tests
+```

+/Users/j/.agents/skills/golang-gopls/SKILL.md
+---
+name: golang-gopls
+description: "Golang semantic code intelligence via `gopls`, the official Go language server — go-to-definition, find references, call/implementation hierarchy, workspace symbol search, package API discovery, diagnostics, safe rename, refactors (extract/inline/fill/rewrite code actions), formatting, and generated tests. Reaches an agent via gopls's own MCP server (`go_*` tools), Claude Code's native `LSP` tool, or the `gopls` CLI. Use when navigating or refactoring Go code — jumping to a definition, finding call sites before a rename, understanding a file's or package's dependencies, running diagnostics after an edit, or extracting/inlining/renaming. Not for the published ecosystem — packages not in your `go.mod`, versions, licenses, importers — → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`). Not for a whole-tree vulnerability audit → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-security` skill (`govulncheck`)."
+user-invocable: true
+license: MIT
+compatibility: Designed for Claude Code, Codex or similar harness. Requires the gopls binary (go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest) v0.20+ on PATH.
+metadata:
+  author: samber
+  version: "1.1.1"
+  openclaw:
+    emoji: "🛰️"
+    homepage: https://github.com/samber/cc-skills-golang
+    requires:
+      bins:
+        - go
+        - gopls
+    install:
+      - kind: go
+        package: golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest
+        bins: [gopls]
+    skill-library-version: "0.22.0"
+allowed-tools: Read Edit Write Glob Grep Bash(go:*) Bash(golangci-lint:*) Bash(git:*) Agent Bash(gopls:*) LSP mcp__gopls__*
+paths:
+  - "**/*.go"
+---
+
+**Persona:** You are a Go engineer who reaches for semantic code intelligence instead of grep whenever a question is about the resolved build — grep finds text, `gopls` finds meaning (types, call graphs, shadowing, implementation relationships).
+
+**Dependencies:** `gopls` — `go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest` (v0.20+). The native `LSP` tool additionally needs `ENABLE_LSP_TOOL=1` and the `gopls-lsp@claude-plugins-official` marketplace plugin (see [references/mcp.md](references/mcp.md)).
+
+`gopls` is the official Go language server. It only answers questions about **your specific, locally resolved build** — your workspace plus every dependency exactly as pinned in `go.sum`, including `replace` directives. For a package that isn't part of that build (versions, docs, licenses, CVEs of something you haven't added yet), → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`) instead.
+
+## Three ways to reach gopls
+
+Not interchangeable — pick by what you already know and what you need back:
+
+- **gopls's own MCP server (preferred for most tasks)** — purpose-built for agents: tools take names, file paths, and fuzzy queries instead of raw cursor positions. Register once per machine: `claude mcp add gopls -- gopls mcp`. Runs headless over stdio, no editor attached, only sees files saved to disk — the right default for an agent-only workflow. See [references/mcp.md](references/mcp.md) for every tool.
+- **The native `LSP` tool** — Claude Code's built-in editor-style integration. Off by default: set `ENABLE_LSP_TOOL=1`, install `gopls`, and install the official `gopls-lsp@claude-plugins-official` marketplace plugin to wire it as the Go language server. Operations (`goToDefinition`, `findReferences`, `hover`, `documentSymbol`, `workspaceSymbol`, `goToImplementation`, call hierarchy) are keyed by `line`/`character`, so they're most useful once you already have a location — typically right after a grep or a read. Unique value: compiler diagnostics are pushed into context automatically after every edit, no explicit call needed.
+- **The `gopls` CLI** — same engine, invoked as `gopls <command> <file:line:col>`. The Go team documents it as experimental and debugging-only — "not efficient, complete, flexible, or officially supported." Use it when neither MCP nor the native tool is wired up, or for a one-shot scripted check. Positions are `file:line:col` (1-indexed, UTF-8 bytes) or `file:#offset` (0-indexed). See [references/cli.md](references/cli.md).
+
+**Preference order: MCP → native `LSP` → CLI.** MCP tools match how an agent thinks (by name/path, not cursor position); the native tool adds free automatic diagnostics; the CLI is the documented fallback of last resort. Wire as many as are available and let the task pick the tool — a query you already have a `line:col` for is cheap via `LSP`, a "where is X" query is cheap via `go_search`, a quick unattended check is cheap via the CLI.
+
+## Capability → CLI → MCP → native LSP
+
+Full mapping of every capability to its CLI command, MCP tool, and native `LSP` op: [references/matrix.md](references/matrix.md).
+
+## Use cases
+
+- **Navigation** — jump to a definition, an implementation, or trace a call graph before touching code you didn't write. Details: [references/features.md](references/features.md#navigation).
+- **Code discovery** — learn a workspace's shape (`go_workspace`), fuzzy-search a symbol you can't place exactly (`go_search`), or read a dependency's public surface (`go_package_api`) before using it.
+- **Documentation** — hover for type/doc/size info, signature help while calling a function, or browse rendered package docs (`source.doc`, including internal packages pkg.go.dev never sees).
+- **Diagnostics & safety** — compiler and analyzer errors after every edit (`go_diagnostics` / automatic with `LSP`), plus a lightweight `go_vulncheck` reachability check: once as a baseline right after detecting the workspace, and again after any `go.mod` change.
+- **Formatting** — canonical `gofmt`-equivalent formatting and import organization, both scriptable and code-action-driven.
+- **Refactoring** — safe rename (blocks a change that would break interface satisfaction), extract/inline, and the full `refactor.rewrite.*` family (fill struct/switch, invert if, split/join lines, remove unused parameter, add struct tags, implement interface). Full catalog with gotchas: [references/features.md](references/features.md#transformation).
+
+## Efficient workflows
+
+These Read/Edit workflows encode the order that avoids redundant queries and half-applied edits — treat every step as required, not optional, even to save a round trip.
+
+- **Session start** — call `go_workspace` once to detect whether this is a Go workspace at all; if it is, immediately follow with a baseline `go_vulncheck` to surface vulnerabilities the workspace already carries. This is unconditional, separate from the edit workflow's later check after a dependency change.
+
+**Read workflow** (understand before touching anything):
+
+1. `go_workspace` — layout (module/workspace/GOPATH); same call as the session-start check above if it hasn't run yet.
+2. `go_search` — fuzzy-locate a type/function/variable by name.
+3. `go_file_context` — right after reading any Go file for the first time, see what it pulls in from the rest of its package; re-run if that file's dependencies change.
+4. `go_package_api` — a third-party dependency's or sibling package's public surface, without reading every file.
+
+**Edit workflow** (iterate until diagnostics are clean):
+
+1. Read first (workflow above).
+2. `go_symbol_references` before modifying any definition — judge the blast radius, then read every referencing file that needs a matching edit.
+3. Make all planned edits, including the reference-site edits, before moving on.
+4. `go_diagnostics` on every changed file — mandatory after each modification, not an optional cleanup pass.
+5. Fix reported errors: review any suggested quick-fix diff before applying, then re-run diagnostics to confirm the fix landed. Ignore hint/info diagnostics unrelated to the task. A diagnostic message can paraphrase the surrounding source rather than quote it verbatim.
+6. Only if `go.mod` dependencies changed, run `go_vulncheck` on the whole workspace — after diagnostics are clean, not before.
+7. Run `go test <changed-package-paths>` — not `./...` unless explicitly asked, since a full-repo run slows the iteration loop.
+
+**Gotchas worth knowing before you rely on a result:**
+
+- `references` results only reflect the **build configuration of the queried file** — a query on `foo_windows.go` will not surface matches in `bar_linux.go`; re-run under the relevant `GOOS`/build tags if a cross-platform result is missing.
+- `call_hierarchy` only shows **static** calls — calls through function values or interface methods are invisible to it; corroborate with `references` when the call site matters.
+- Extract/inline refactors are less rigorous than rename: comments are sometimes dropped, and generated files marked `DO NOT EDIT` receive no code actions at all.
+- `refactor.rewrite.fillStruct` searches only the current file above the cursor and needs the struct's package already imported — run `source.organizeImports` first if the type was just typed in.
+
+## gopls vs godig vs Context7 vs govulncheck
+
+`gopls` only reasons about code present and resolvable in the local build:
+
+- For anything not tied to that build (version history, license, ecosystem-wide importers, CVEs of a package not yet added) → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`) — it queries pkg.go.dev directly, no local checkout needed.
+- For a comprehensive, whole-tree vulnerability audit (CI gates, periodic sweeps) rather than gopls's lightweight on-demand `go_vulncheck` → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-security` skill (`govulncheck`).
+- Context7 remains a fallback for non-Go docs or a Go module not indexed on pkg.go.dev.
+
+The full task-to-tool matrix lives in the `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-how-to` skill's "`godig` vs gopls vs Context7 vs govulncheck" section.

+## Execution discipline (owner feedback, 2026-10-12: "slow, divergent, repeats work")
+
+Evidence rules above set the *standard*, not the *volume*. Scale verification to
+risk (docs/copy/tests: targeted tests; service code: module suite + the CI lens,
+i.e. the Linux container; money/auth/migrations/prod/fleet: full gate + prod
+read-back). Land a verified batch at least every 90 minutes. Every action maps to a
+checklist item; out-of-scope findings go to a one-line "Parked" list unless P0.
+Reuse evidence already recorded in the round doc for the same object and
+revision; do not re-measure or re-read unchanged material. One lane per question,
+and a darwin-skipped test is not a passed test.
+
+The following owner rules are tracked here so a fresh checkout does not depend
+on a workstation-only `~/.codex/AGENTS.md`:
+
+- Deliver a small, single-topic, verifiable batch within 90 minutes. If blocked,
+  record the blocker and expected resolution in the round log. Ready fixes do
+  not wait for wider audits.
+- Name the checklist item for each action. Only P0 incidents (production outage,
+  data loss or an actively exploited vulnerability) may interrupt the scope.
+- Reuse evidence for the same object and revision. Remeasure only when the
+  object changes, evidence exceeds 24 hours, or evidence conflicts. Do not reread
+  whole files already read in the session. After two identical command failures,
+  investigate the cause or change approach before retrying.
+- Report status changes: what changed, where the evidence is, and what is next.
+  Close an item once its acceptance criteria are met; do not keep polishing it.
+- Use exact-path staging in each independent repository. Do not overwrite
+  another session's work or change an executing script's bytes.
+- Preserve owner-opened interactive sessions. Coordination, takeover, an idle
+  screen or a release-lock conflict does not authorize signaling, closing or
+  injecting input into the owner's Grok, Codex, Claude or terminal session.
+  Stop only a worker this session created, or one explicitly transferred to
+  this session, with a verified receipt (session ID, PID and start time); an
+  owner instruction to stop the specific session also authorizes it. Reading
+  another lane's receipt does not transfer ownership. Otherwise record the
+  conflict in the existing inbox and keep independent work moving. See
+  `yueto-orchestration` for the process-ownership boundary.

+  and explanatory comments use English. Preserve product translations, exact

+/Users/j/Projects/yueto/CLAUDE.md
+# CLAUDE.md
+
+@AGENTS.md
+
+Shared workspace guidance for Claude Code and Codex (`AGENTS.md` loads this file).
+
+**All owner-facing replies must be in Chinese**, including progress updates,
+tables and final reports. Keep code identifiers, commands and SHAs unchanged.
+Repository engineering documentation and explanatory comments use English.
+
+This is the **`yue.to` workspace root** — an umbrella over a family of independent git repos that together make up a privatized (from-Xboard) VPN/proxy service: panel, node fleet, client apps, and ops. It is NOT a monorepo. Each subdirectory is its own repository; the root repo tracks nine directories — `docs/`, `scripts/`, `design/`, `i18n/`, `schedule/`, `help/`, `.claude/` (skills/rules/agents/hooks/settings only; `worktrees/`/`projects/`/`state/` stay ignored), `.agents/` (Codex skill links), `.grok/` (Grok rules, LSP, and three skill overrides; estate skills stay in `.claude/skills/`) — plus `.codex/hooks.json` and a few top-level files: `yueto.code-workspace`, `.gitleaksignore`, and the signed release desired state `release.yaml` + `release.yaml.asc` (`.gitignore` is `/*/` + allowlist, so root *files* are not ignored). Root `reports/` is a local, untracked scratch area — never commit it or cite it as current state. Most real work happens **inside** a component repo, each of which has its own richer `CLAUDE.md` — read that one too when working in it.
+
+## Where the knowledge lives: this file, skills, rules
+
+This file is **always-loaded context** and is kept under the official ~200-line target. Everything
+else has a home that loads on demand — that is the whole design, not a filing convenience:
+
+- **`.claude/skills/` — workspace skills**, symlinked into `~/.claude/skills/` so they load
+  through `.agents/skills/` for Codex and installed for both agents by `scripts/sync-agent-skills.py --apply --user`. Procedural knowledge: worthless most of the time, and must be
+  word-for-word correct the rest of the time.
+- **`.claude/rules/` — path-scoped rules** (`paths:` frontmatter). A rule enters context only when
+  a file it names is read. Use it for "this *file class* has a trap", never for prose.
+- **`.claude/agents/` — project subagents**: `component-worker` (edits a component repo from its own
+  worktree under root `.claude/worktrees/` — never `isolation: worktree`) and `readonly-investigator`.
+
+| Skill (routes by **domain**) | Load it when |
+|---|---|
+| `guard-authoring` | changing/reviewing guard coverage, contract checks, CI assertions or probes; diagnosing test import/fixture leakage; ordinary fixes that only run existing checks do not trigger it |
+| `release-chain` | planning or running any release, image roll, promote or production deploy |
+| `node-lifecycle` | classifying maintenance/retirement or supplier-quota availability; provisioning, cloning, rebuilding or hard-deleting a fleet node |
+| `fleet-diagnose` | diagnosing node/fleet/network/latency/loss/resource problems, **before** changing production |
+| `pg-safety` | writing a Postgres migration, GRANT/role manifest, cross-ownership table access, erasure change, or a spend/eligibility query |
+| `generated-blocks` | changing design tokens, user-facing strings/translations, help articles, or any recurring job |
+| `round-closeout` | closing out an audit / optimization / incident round |
+| `yueto-orchestration` | running an authorized multi-session round under the current coordinator (Claude or Codex): one release exit, side-branch candidates, inbox, release train and shutdown; no fixed-model approval prerequisite |
+
+| Skill (routes by **action**) | Load it when |
+|---|---|
+| `yueto-agent-maintenance` | equipping, configuring or diagnosing coding-agent capabilities for this workspace |
+| `evidence-discipline` | interpreting operational measurements or asserting production/fleet health or runtime improvement; match the claim to its evidence |
+| `landed-not-written` | installing executable scripts/configuration/automation or claiming an installed change took effect; verify the actual consumer |
+| `change-blast-radius` | removing a boundary, renaming, moving a file, zeroing a parameter, dropping a wait, or touching a constant other code pins |
+| `concurrent-git-safety` | committing/pushing, spawning concurrent agents into one repo, or editing anything while a long-running script executes |
+| `patrol` | routine read-only patrol of failed units, DNS sentinel receipts, relay-pool evidence and local workload admission; never changes production |
+| `readonly-reconcile` | read-only balance/points reconciliation or release accounting comparisons; never repairs data |
+| `release-readback` | after a signed release: reconcile promoted, desired and running images, migration receipts and before/after accounting evidence |
+
+Select skills by the actual change and claim. Related-skill links are conditional,
+not a recursive reading list. Reuse loaded guidance and read only relevant reference sections.
+
+Component repos use the same mechanism: **yueops** nine (`yueops-agent-deploy`, `-host-hardening`, `-deploy-gotchas`,
+`-panel-db-gotchas`, `-business-gotchas`, `-emby`, `-ai-support`, `-telegram`, `-business-features`) and **yuelink**
+five (`yuelink-platform-gotchas`, `-core-ffi`, `-panel-api`, `-environment`, `-release-ci`), moved **verbatim** from each `CLAUDE.md`.
+
+**Measure before proposing another split** (`wc -l` + tokens over the real byte/CJK mix, same unit on
+both sides; dated readings live in `round-closeout`). **yueboard and yue-node are at target — do not split them.**
+🚨 Moving doc text into a skill silently shrinks the scan surface of **negative** doc-truth guards
+("must no longer say Y") while they stay green. 📎 `change-blast-radius`.
+
+`.claude/hooks/` (wired in `settings.json`, tests beside them): `yueto-notice.py` reminds, never
+blocks, on a `git push` touching yueboard or an edit to a generated token file, `schedule/registry.yaml`
+or an i18n catalog; `session-status.py` (SessionStart) injects any side branch / dirty tree / unpushed
+commit / pin mismatch / release lock — UNKNOWN, never "clean". `defaultMode: "bypassPermissions"` is
+the owner's 2026-08-31 decision: **zero permission prompts** (`ask-gate.py` is deliberately un-wired;
+don't re-wire it); 2026-10-13 extended to subagents (`permissionMode` + a `PermissionRequest` auto-allow). The one
+blocking hook is `secret-read-guard.py` (2026-10-17: denies unredacted secret-file/container-env reads); else reminders, rules, guards.
+
+**Owner decision — 2026-09-10:** never run local download-and-verify acceptance after YueLink
+releases; CI package/runtime checks, artifact verification, signed provenance and the promotion result suffice.
+
+## Workspace commands (root scope)
+
+```bash
+scripts/status-all.sh          # branch / dirty / unpushed for every component repo (+ yuelink/core/mihomo)
+scripts/pull-all.sh            # git pull --ff-only across all; repos with local changes fetch-only
+scripts/check-all-contracts.sh # run every cross-cutting guard, print one summary (--list to see them)
+scripts/gauntlet-launch.sh     # clean login environment, fail-fast admission, exclusive log
+                               # THE pre-release gate: every private repo's own CI stages + all contracts
+                               # (run it only under heavy-run; a direct scripts/gauntlet.sh exits 2)
+scripts/release-state.py       # read-only: source HEAD vs reviewed pin vs promoted image vs production
+scripts/ship.sh <yueops|yueboard|yue-node> [--auto|--no-deploy|--detach|--from-promoted]
+                               # the whole release chain unattended: build → promote → verify → deploy
+scripts/kick-builds.sh [--dry-run]     # trigger yueto-ci poll-sources now instead of waiting for its cron
+scripts/scan-workspace-secrets.sh      # yueops' secret scanner over ALL private repos + root (in gauntlet)
+scripts/run-yueboard-pg18-gauntlet.sh  # release-only yueboard integration gate on a disposable PG18
+scripts/linux-lens-gate.sh             # yueops full suite in the central-CI Linux lens; proves darwin-skipped tests ran
+scripts/disk-hygiene.sh --check|--apply  # the ONLY sanctioned worktree/docker/cache cleanup (keep-list + safety criteria)
+scripts/heavy-run.sh / new-session.sh  # orchestration entry points (semaphore, Codex sessions) 📎 `yueto-orchestration`
+```
+
+The root's own tests are the guard self-tests (`scripts/test-*.py`, `.claude/hooks/test_*.py`), run
+from the workspace venv `~/.venvs/yueto` (3.13 + pytest + pyyaml; system `python3` is 3.9 with neither).
+Hyphenated `test-*.py` names escape pytest discovery — `pytest scripts` silently collects nothing:
+
+```bash
+~/.venvs/yueto/bin/python -m pytest -q scripts/test-root-doc-truth.py                        # one guard's tests
+~/.venvs/yueto/bin/python -m pytest -q scripts/test-schedule-registry.py -k baseline_is_green # one case
+~/.venvs/yueto/bin/python -m pytest -q scripts/test-*.py .claude/hooks                        # all root self-tests
+```
+
+- `ship.sh` only chains the audited per-step scripts, scraping digests from the *promote* step's log
+  (never the build's). 📎 constraints: **`release-chain`**; commands: `docs/release-chain-runbook.md`.
+- `release-state.py` names the next action per row; `--fleet`/`--packages` sweep the fleet, where an
+  unreachable row is **UNKNOWN, never OK**. 📎 `release-chain`.
+- `gauntlet.sh` is the only fail-closed "did everything actually run" gate; `--fast` drops the
+  network stages and **does not qualify for a release**. 📎 `guard-authoring`.
+- `status-all.sh`/`pull-all.sh` also cover `yuelink/core/mihomo`, a *separate* repo inside `yuelink`.
+
+🚨 **Before believing a red guard or a four-truths mismatch, run `scripts/status-all.sh`.** Cross-repo
+guards read each repo's **main checkout**, so whatever branch it is on is a hidden input (2026-09-06:
+one repo left on a side branch → 8 red contracts, 6 pure artifacts). 📎 `concurrent-git-safety`.
+
+`check-all-contracts.sh` is the one place that knows the **full** guard set (`--list`; never copy the
+count). Every comparator that reads two repos at once runs **here and nowhere else** — single-repo CI
+lacks the sibling checkout. 📎 `guard-authoring` (incl. the `guard-ci-claims` meta-guard).
+
+## The cross-repo sources of truth the root repo itself owns
+
+Four small root directories are the **single upstream** for values duplicated across five surfaces
+and four runtimes — so an edit inside a component repo to any generated block is a regression, not a fix.
+
+- **`design/tokens/yue.tokens.json`** — the design scale. `node design/build-tokens.mjs` regenerates
+  one fenced block per surface plus the scale tables in `docs/yue-visual-language-2026.md` §2.
+- **`i18n/catalog/*.json`** — the zh-CN → en catalog; `scripts/i18n/CONTRACT.md` is the one binding spec.
+- **`schedule/registry.yaml`** — the generated inventory of every recurring job across six layers,
+  from `scripts/schedule/extract.py --write`.
+- **`help/articles/*.md`** — the unified help center (`help/CONTRACT.md`); UIs reference `help:<slug>`.
+
+📎 **Before touching any of these, read the `generated-blocks` skill** (generated vs. hand-written,
+fence rules, never-translate set, ratchets).
+
+## The system: four planes
+
+Data flows **panel → subscription → client**, and **panel ⇄ node** for control.
+
+| Repo | Plane | Stack | Role |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| **yueboard** | Control (panel) | Go (chi + sqlc + goose + pgx/PG, ConnectRPC) | Native panel backend for user/admin APIs, login, **subscription generation**, and the only node control-plane server. The former REST/WS node protocol and fallback proxy are gone. Native admin/user SPAs (React 19) are embedded with `go:embed`; `my.yue.to` runs this. |
+| **yue-node** | Data (nodes) | Go (`cmd/` + `internal/…`, kernels as adapters) | Native node agent on the anti-GFW fleet, with direct hosts and shared relays. **Never restate the fleet size from memory or from this file**: the three counts (direct hosts / serving assignments / unique enabled nodes) come only from the live inventory (`servers` where `deploy_mode='direct'`; 📎 `.claude/rules/fleet-baseline.md`); `docs/yue-product-surface-inventory-2026.md` carries only a **dated snapshot** (dated, self-consistent, pointing at the live source — a second hand-copy caused the 2026-08-18 guard that *refused* to let a stale fact be corrected). Runs Reality/VLESS and Hysteria2 and talks to yueboard only through machine-scoped ConnectRPC. `/home/yue-node` on each machine. |
+| **yuelink** | Client | Flutter UI + mihomo (Clash.Meta) Go core via FFI | The Yue.to cross-platform app (Android/iOS/macOS/Windows/Linux, id `com.yueto.yuelink`). The server template is authoritative; the client adds only bounded compatibility defaults and seeds bundled `.mrs` rule sets for offline startup. |
+| **yueops** | Ops / services | Python (FastAPI, python-telegram-bot, RAG) monorepo | Supporting services: `checkin-api` (check-in), `yue-bot`/yubot (Telegram) + TG mini-app, AI support (Xiaoyue; RAG uses API embeddings and in-process NumPy cosine similarity, not pgvector), DNS/Cloudflare automation, telemetry ingest. Deployed as containers on the bastion. |
+
+Supporting repos: **yuelink-ci** / **yueto-ci** are public build-only CI repos (they began as a workaround for the private-repo Actions billing freeze, which comes and goes — see the CI section — but `yueto-ci` has since become load-bearing for a supply-chain reason that holds regardless); **hysteria**, **Xray-core** and **quic-go** are the maintained data-plane kernel forks (yue-node's HY2 and Reality/VLESS engines plus HY2's QUIC dependency — each carries real yue-specific fixes, so "just track upstream" is wrong there); **clash-verge-rev / ClashMetaForAndroid / clashmi** are historical Yue product forks, frozen migration-only and no longer checked out in this workspace (status: `docs/yue-product-surface-inventory-2026.md`). **yuetube-video** is the private Remotion project that renders the Yue.to QA promo video (Node 24 / Remotion pinned, `src/data/video-script.json` is the single source for copy, subtitles, shot lengths and audio SHA-256; `npm run verify` gates supply chain + assets + `tsc`). It ships no runtime and is coupled to nothing else here. The old PHP panel and its local repository are retired and must not be reintroduced as a runtime, build, template, or fallback dependency.
+
+## Cross-repo coupling that isn't visible in any single repo
+
+- **Subscription generation is a contract between yueboard and every client family.** `yueboard/internal/modules/subscribe/` renders Clash Meta, Surge, Loon, Quantumult X, Surfboard, sing-box, and general links from native templates. There is no `?yl=1` or device-specific server branch. When one client fails, render its exact User-Agent and compare fields against a working renderer before changing the fleet.
+- **The server template is the YueLink source of truth.** YueLink no longer inlines `GeoIP.dat`/`GeoSite.dat`; it seeds the exact bundled `.mrs` set and preserves server routing. Keep client transforms bounded to compatibility defaults, and use the reference clients to justify any divergence.
+- **The schema is native; remaining cross-service reads are explicit migration debt.** Tables use native names, `numeric` amounts, `jsonb`, and `timestamptz`. YueBoard owns panel-domain writes. Any temporary YueOps read must be catalogued and moved behind a scoped native internal API before its DB credential is removed.
+- **Panel↔node control is ConnectRPC only.** The protobuf contract is shared by yueboard and yue-node. Every physical host must use a machine-scoped credential and exact assignment; a fleet-wide token, REST polling, or WS push is a migration regression.
+- **🚨 Never rebuild a node by copying another node's `/home/yue-node` wholesale.** The runtime tree
+  contains `node-*/data/report-outbox`, which is the machine's **billing identity**. Cloning it either
+  double-bills or wedges the node forever on `report sequence gap`, which has no recovery path. This
+  has now happened twice (2026-08-01, 2026-08-11). **Wipe `node-*/data/report-outbox` before first
+  start** — the in-code owner guard only protects hosts already running yue-node `4bf9ada` or newer.
+  📎 Full provisioning / cloning / retiring / hard-delete procedure: the **`node-lifecycle`** skill.
+

+## Release / CI: what is true in every session
+
+Four things must name the same commit: the reviewed **pin**, the source repo **HEAD**, the
+**promoted** image, and what **production** actually runs. `release-state.py` prints all four.
+- **2026-10-12: SSH authority is ACTIVE.** Every new yueops revision needs auto-pull + `make-release-root.sh
+  --strict` + `scripts/ship-sshd-authority.py --apply` before Ops/panel deploys or A/B publishing (ship.sh chains them).
+- **2026-10-11: bastion is CPU-steal-bound** (60–78%): no heavy tests there; check `vmstat` st first. 📎 `release-chain`.
+
+**Before pushing `yueboard/master`, establish its effect on the active release.**
+Promotion requires default-branch ancestry, inputs identical to HEAD and a candidate
+not older than `:latest`. A changed input can invalidate a running release.
+When inputs change, the integration owner must complete these steps:
+
+1. Read the revision labels off the **running production** panel image and node fleet, then run
+   `scripts/converge-yueboard-release-pin.py --from-head --deployed-board-revision <prod-board>
+   --deployed-node-revision <prod-node>`. Both are explicit because reviewed candidates may never
+   have been deployed. It updates yueto-ci's two copies atomically and **derives** the schema
+   floor from `git show <rev>:schema-floor.txt`; since P2 §4 A3 it no longer writes yueops.
+2. Coordinate the required rebuild and panel deployment with the release owner.
+   The desired state yueops deploys from is the **signed** root `release.yaml`
+   (ship.sh writes + seals it; no pin is committed back into yueops). 📎 `release-chain`.
+
+Promote, `release-state.py` and the schema-migrator contract accept a descendant
+

+-// 管理端凭据重置的审计回归（P2）。
+-//
+-// `{admin}/user/resetSecret` 的审计行原来写在函数末尾，而 202 的 pending 分支
+-// 在它之前就 return 了；剩下的同步分支还被 `receipt.Operation.Created` 二次收窄。

+# Guard authoring (Yue.to)
+
+The expensive failure is a green guard protecting nothing. The linked incident examples
+occurred in actual production work.
+
+Apply the checklist to the guard's actual inputs, dependencies and claimed
+coverage. Running an existing check does not require redesigning it. A changed
+assertion, scan boundary or success/failure classification does require this skill.
+For import/fixture leakage, load [test isolation](references/test-isolation.md)
+first; use the guard checklist only if guard coverage changes.
+
+Before adding an invariant, establish which consumer needs it and check its
+actual semantics. Challenge it with one realistic counterexample at that boundary:
+subprocess execution for locks, a write failure for persistence, or raw single-target
+output for collectors. Matching strings or unrelated runner-image labels cannot
+establish those properties. Reuse the recorded interpreter/image, user and mounts;
+classify failures as startup, collection or assertion before editing product code.
+
+## Establish three facts before accepting green
+
+1. Did this run reach the target? Zero executions is not a pass.
+2. Did the scan cover the intended objects? Zero matches is not universal success.
+3. Is the guarded layer the layer users and production actually encounter?
+
+A negative answer makes the green result meaningless.
+
+## Load only the relevant incident reference
+
+Keep the common criteria and checklist below for guard changes. Read a reference
+only when its failure class applies; the catalog is retained evidence, not an
+extra audit assignment.
+
+| Observed problem | Reference |
+|---|---|
+| A check never executes, scans nothing, targets the wrong layer, hides failure, or has no legitimate success path | [Coverage failures](references/coverage-failures.md), numbered incidents 1–9 |
+| Wrong interpreter or checkout, SSH stdin, toolchain drift, signed installation closure, systemd results, incomplete pagination or runtime budgets | [Execution boundaries](references/execution-boundaries.md) |
+| Import/fixture pollution, swallowed real I/O timeouts, fork deadlocks or teardown order | [Test isolation](references/test-isolation.md) |
+
+## Required guard-delivery checklist
+
+- [ ] Remove/rename/delete the protected object in an isolated mutation: the
+  guard must fail, not mistake missing input for correctness.
+- [ ] Exercise dependency failures the guard can encounter: wrong credentials
+  for authenticated probes, network failure for remote probes, and permission
+  failure for protected inputs. Verify their visible classifications.
+- [ ] For scans, require a minimum target count; for a single target, prove arrival.
+- [ ] Before counting negative observations, prove target-handler arrival and
+  run controls protecting legitimate behavior.
+- [ ] Register cross-repository guards in `scripts/check-all-contracts.sh`.
+  `<path>` must name the sibling file actually read, not the guard script itself;
+  otherwise missing-repository SKIP is unreachable. `guard-ci-claims` enforces it.
+- [ ] Enumerate every criterion dimension, such as status × amount × ownership.
+- [ ] Never duplicate live fleet counts. Since 2026-09-14, derive them from
+  `servers.deploy_mode='direct'`; node-control-auth-migrate.py EXPECTED baselines
+  no longer exist. Documentation may retain dated snapshots. Only mechanically
+  enforced authoritative projections and date-anchored observations are exceptions.
+
+## Workspace-only guards
+
+`scripts/check-all-contracts.sh` alone knows the complete cross-repository guard
+set. Single-repo CI cannot see all private siblings; a green component does not
+establish a shared contract. The registry explicitly states whether a component
+CI consumer exists.
+
+A 2026-08-02 isolated Ops checkout showed whole tests skipped in ci-backend.yml:
+`panel-deep-links` (2), `idempotency-catalog` (5, module-level pytestmark),
+`registry-links` (2), and cross-surface design-token-gate cases. These and the
+root scripts-level comparators run only in the workspace. Run the contract script
+before pushing cross-repository changes.
+
+`guard-ci-claims` (`scripts/check-guard-ci-claims.py`) fails when sibling-dependent
+entries lack workspace-only marking or claimed component CI lacks a workflow
+execution path. Missing repositories must report SKIP; `<path>` identifies the
+actual sibling input, never the guard itself, so missing input cannot appear PASS.
+
+`scripts/gauntlet.sh` similarly turns a former verbal pre-release promise into
+execution: missing tools/repos are SKIP, never PASS, and too few actually executed
+stages fail the gate.

+name: golang-gopls
+description: "Golang semantic code intelligence via `gopls`, the official Go language server — go-to-definition, find references, call/implementation hierarchy, workspace symbol search, package API discovery, diagnostics, safe rename, refactors (extract/inline/fill/rewrite code actions), formatting, and generated tests. Reaches an agent via gopls's own MCP server (`go_*` tools), Claude Code's native `LSP` tool, or the `gopls` CLI. Use when navigating or refactoring Go code — jumping to a definition, finding call sites before a rename, understanding a file's or package's dependencies, running diagnostics after an edit, or extracting/inlining/renaming. Not for the published ecosystem — packages not in your `go.mod`, versions, licenses, importers — → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`). Not for a whole-tree vulnerability audit → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-security` skill (`govulncheck`)."
+user-invocable: true
+license: MIT
+compatibility: Designed for Claude Code, Codex or similar harness. Requires the gopls binary (go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest) v0.20+ on PATH.

+
+**Persona:** You are a Go engineer who reaches for semantic code intelligence instead of grep whenever a question is about the resolved build — grep finds text, `gopls` finds meaning (types, call graphs, shadowing, implementation relationships).
+
+**Dependencies:** `gopls` — `go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@latest` (v0.20+). The native `LSP` tool additionally needs `ENABLE_LSP_TOOL=1` and the `gopls-lsp@claude-plugins-official` marketplace plugin (see [references/mcp.md](references/mcp.md)).
+
+`gopls` is the official Go language server. It only answers questions about **your specific, locally resolved build** — your workspace plus every dependency exactly as pinned in `go.sum`, including `replace` directives. For a package that isn't part of that build (versions, docs, licenses, CVEs of something you haven't added yet), → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`) instead.
+
+## Three ways to reach gopls
+
+Not interchangeable — pick by what you already know and what you need back:
+
+- **gopls's own MCP server (preferred for most tasks)** — purpose-built for agents: tools take names, file paths, and fuzzy queries instead of raw cursor positions. Register once per machine: `claude mcp add gopls -- gopls mcp`. Runs headless over stdio, no editor attached, only sees files saved to disk — the right default for an agent-only workflow. See [references/mcp.md](references/mcp.md) for every tool.
+- **The native `LSP` tool** — Claude Code's built-in editor-style integration. Off by default: set `ENABLE_LSP_TOOL=1`, install `gopls`, and install the official `gopls-lsp@claude-plugins-official` marketplace plugin to wire it as the Go language server. Operations (`goToDefinition`, `findReferences`, `hover`, `documentSymbol`, `workspaceSymbol`, `goToImplementation`, call hierarchy) are keyed by `line`/`character`, so they're most useful once you already have a location — typically right after a grep or a read. Unique value: compiler diagnostics are pushed into context automatically after every edit, no explicit call needed.
+- **The `gopls` CLI** — same engine, invoked as `gopls <command> <file:line:col>`. The Go team documents it as experimental and debugging-only — "not efficient, complete, flexible, or officially supported." Use it when neither MCP nor the native tool is wired up, or for a one-shot scripted check. Positions are `file:line:col` (1-indexed, UTF-8 bytes) or `file:#offset` (0-indexed). See [references/cli.md](references/cli.md).
+
+**Preference order: MCP → native `LSP` → CLI.** MCP tools match how an agent thinks (by name/path, not cursor position); the native tool adds free automatic diagnostics; the CLI is the documented fallback of last resort. Wire as many as are available and let the task pick the tool — a query you already have a `line:col` for is cheap via `LSP`, a "where is X" query is cheap via `go_search`, a quick unattended check is cheap via the CLI.
+
+## Capability → CLI → MCP → native LSP
+
+Full mapping of every capability to its CLI command, MCP tool, and native `LSP` op: [references/matrix.md](references/matrix.md).
+
+## Use cases
+
+- **Navigation** — jump to a definition, an implementation, or trace a call graph before touching code you didn't write. Details: [references/features.md](references/features.md#navigation).
+- **Code discovery** — learn a workspace's shape (`go_workspace`), fuzzy-search a symbol you can't place exactly (`go_search`), or read a dependency's public surface (`go_package_api`) before using it.
+- **Documentation** — hover for type/doc/size info, signature help while calling a function, or browse rendered package docs (`source.doc`, including internal packages pkg.go.dev never sees).
+- **Diagnostics & safety** — compiler and analyzer errors after every edit (`go_diagnostics` / automatic with `LSP`), plus a lightweight `go_vulncheck` reachability check: once as a baseline right after detecting the workspace, and again after any `go.mod` change.
+- **Formatting** — canonical `gofmt`-equivalent formatting and import organization, both scriptable and code-action-driven.
+- **Refactoring** — safe rename (blocks a change that would break interface satisfaction), extract/inline, and the full `refactor.rewrite.*` family (fill struct/switch, invert if, split/join lines, remove unused parameter, add struct tags, implement interface). Full catalog with gotchas: [references/features.md](references/features.md#transformation).
+
+## Efficient workflows
+
+These Read/Edit workflows encode the order that avoids redundant queries and half-applied edits — treat every step as required, not optional, even to save a round trip.
+
+- **Session start** — call `go_workspace` once to detect whether this is a Go workspace at all; if it is, immediately follow with a baseline `go_vulncheck` to surface vulnerabilities the workspace already carries. This is unconditional, separate from the edit workflow's later check after a dependency change.
+
+**Read workflow** (understand before touching anything):
+
+1. `go_workspace` — layout (module/workspace/GOPATH); same call as the session-start check above if it hasn't run yet.
+2. `go_search` — fuzzy-locate a type/function/variable by name.
+3. `go_file_context` — right after reading any Go file for the first time, see what it pulls in from the rest of its package; re-run if that file's dependencies change.
+4. `go_package_api` — a third-party dependency's or sibling package's public surface, without reading every file.
+
+**Edit workflow** (iterate until diagnostics are clean):
+
+1. Read first (workflow above).
+2. `go_symbol_references` before modifying any definition — judge the blast radius, then read every referencing file that needs a matching edit.
+3. Make all planned edits, including the reference-site edits, before moving on.
+4. `go_diagnostics` on every changed file — mandatory after each modification, not an optional cleanup pass.
+5. Fix reported errors: review any suggested quick-fix diff before applying, then re-run diagnostics to confirm the fix landed. Ignore hint/info diagnostics unrelated to the task. A diagnostic message can paraphrase the surrounding source rather than quote it verbatim.
+6. Only if `go.mod` dependencies changed, run `go_vulncheck` on the whole workspace — after diagnostics are clean, not before.
+7. Run `go test <changed-package-paths>` — not `./...` unless explicitly asked, since a full-repo run slows the iteration loop.
+
+**Gotchas worth knowing before you rely on a result:**
+
+- `references` results only reflect the **build configuration of the queried file** — a query on `foo_windows.go` will not surface matches in `bar_linux.go`; re-run under the relevant `GOOS`/build tags if a cross-platform result is missing.
+- `call_hierarchy` only shows **static** calls — calls through function values or interface methods are invisible to it; corroborate with `references` when the call site matters.
+- Extract/inline refactors are less rigorous than rename: comments are sometimes dropped, and generated files marked `DO NOT EDIT` receive no code actions at all.
+- `refactor.rewrite.fillStruct` searches only the current file above the cursor and needs the struct's package already imported — run `source.organizeImports` first if the type was just typed in.
+
+## gopls vs godig vs Context7 vs govulncheck
+
+`gopls` only reasons about code present and resolvable in the local build:
+
+- For anything not tied to that build (version history, license, ecosystem-wide importers, CVEs of a package not yet added) → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-pkg-go-dev` skill (`godig`) — it queries pkg.go.dev directly, no local checkout needed.
+- For a comprehensive, whole-tree vulnerability audit (CI gates, periodic sweeps) rather than gopls's lightweight on-demand `go_vulncheck` → See `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-security` skill (`govulncheck`).
+- Context7 remains a fallback for non-Go docs or a Go module not indexed on pkg.go.dev.
+
+The full task-to-tool matrix lives in the `samber/cc-skills-golang@golang-how-to` skill's "`godig` vs gopls vs Context7 vs govulncheck" section.
